**SN and its closure properties.**

Define `sn := strongly_normalizing`.

Wait, strong normalization: SN t := all reduction paths finite, equivalently well-founded. The inductive definition SN_intro: t is SN if all one-step successors are SN. That's the standard accessibility. So properties:

- If all t' with t --> t' are SN, then SN t.
- Inverse: if SN t and t --> t', then SN t' (inversion on SN_intro). Easy.

So to prove new terms SN, need show all their reducts SN. For application t1 t2, reducts might be many.

Let's define substitution on fvars:

```
Fixpoint subst_f (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst_f sigma t1) (subst_f sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T body => tm_abs T (subst_f sigma body)
  | ...
  end.
```

`R_expand : forall T t t', t --> t' -> (forall t'', t --> t'' -> t'' = t') -> R T t' -> R T t.`

Now, the fundamental theorem and substitution machinery. Let's carefully define everything.

**Substitution on free variables.**

```
Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  end.
```

5. Value→lc: v_abs has lc; v_true/v_false: lc at any k. Easy.

6. Now the main relation. Let's define:

```
Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => lc_at 0 t /\ strongly_normalizing t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => lc_at 0 t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall v, value v -> R T1 v -> R T2 (tm_app t v))
  end.
```

Note R appears in negative position? In the arrow case, R T1 and R T2 with T1, T2 structurally smaller, so the Fixpoint is structurally recursive on T. Inside, R T2 appears as an argument to a function type? The definition `R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t := ... forall v, value v -> R T1 v -> R T2 (tm_app t v)` — this is a recursive call on smaller type, fine for Fixpoint since it's a Gallina term. Coq accepts nested recursive occurrences in Prop as long as structural on T. Let's test.

Now lemmas:

(1) `R_sn : forall T t, R T t -> strongly_normalizing t`. By destruct T; simpl; tauto/intros.

(2) `R_lc : forall T t, R T t -> lc_at 0 t`. Similar.

(3) `sn_step : forall t t', strongly_normalizing t -> t --> t' -> strongly_normalizing t'`.
Proof: intros t t' H. induction H as [t Hall]. intros Hs. apply Hall; auto. Actually inversion H: H : strongly_normalizing t with constructor SN_intro: H = SN_intro t Hall. `inversion H; subst; auto.` Let's write:
```
Lemma sn_step : forall t t', strongly_normalizing t -> step t t' -> strongly_normalizing t'.
Proof. intros t t' Hsn Hstep. inversion Hsn; subst. apply H0. exact Hstep. Qed.
```
But inversion might produce H0 : forall t', step t t' -> sn t'. Fine.

(4) `sn_abs : forall T t, strongly_normalizing (tm_abs T t)`, `sn_true`, `sn_false`. Proof: constructor; intros t' H; inversion H. But wait, inversion on step where source is tm_abs: does Coq derive False? Step constructors: ST_AppAbs conclusion `tm_app (tm_abs T t) v --> open t v` — the source is tm_app, cannot unify with tm_abs. ST_App1/App2 sources tm_app. So yes, no unification. But Coq's `inversion H` needs H : tm_abs T t --> t'. It should solve by discriminating constructors. Let's verify in practice.

Actually a subtlety: `step` is an Inductive with reserved notation; constructors named ST_AppAbs etc. Inversion should work.

(5) `value_lc : forall v, value v -> lc_at 0 v`.
Proof: induction 1; [assumption | constructor | constructor]. Wait lc_true : forall k, lc_at k tm_true; use k=0.

(6) `value_no_step : forall v, value v -> forall t', ~ (v --> t')`.

Hmm, `inversion Hv` may give v_abs etc. Better: `intros v Hv; induction Hv; intros t' Hstep; inversion Hstep.` Should work.

(7) `lc_at_mono : forall k k', k <= k' -> forall t, lc_at k t -> lc_at k' t`. Induction on t; lc_at inversion. Or structural on lc_at derivation: `induction 2`? We can prove by induction on the lc_at derivation: `intros k k' Hle t H; induction H; subst...` but k is fixed in each constructor? Let's write:
```
Lemma lc_at_mono : forall k k' t, k <= k' -> lc_at k t -> lc_at k' t.
Proof.
  intros k k' t Hle H. revert k' Hle.
  induction H; intros k' Hle.
  - apply lc_bvar. lia.  (i < k <= k')
  - apply lc_fvar.
  - apply lc_app; auto.
  - apply lc_abs. apply IHlc_at. lia.
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
Qed.
```
Need lia from Arith.PeanoNat? `lia` is in Lia (Stdlib.micromega.Lia). PeanoNat gives Nat.le. We can import Lia: `From Stdlib Require Import Lia.` Allowed (locally installed standard library). The task says use supplied task and locally installed Rocq libraries; adding imports from Stdlib is fine.

Actually, careful about modifying "supplied language definitions": adding imports and definitions/lemmas is allowed.

(8) `open_lc : forall k u t, lc_at k u -> lc_at (S k) t -> lc_at k (open_rec k u t)`.
Proof induction on t:
- bvar i: open_rec k u (bvar i) = if eqb k i then u else bvar i. Case eqb k i true → i=k → lc_at k u. Case false → i≠k; from lc_at (S k) (bvar i) have i < S k; need i < k. Since i ≠ k, i < k by lia. Need `Nat.eqb k i = false -> k ≠ i`; using Nat.eqb_eq. We can destruct (Nat.eqb k i) eqn:E; apply Nat.eqb_eq in E.
- fvar: lc_fvar.
- app: IH.
- abs: lc_at (S k) (abs T body) → lc_at (S(S k)) body; IH with k:=S k gives lc_at (S k) (open_rec (S k) u body), then lc_abs with k gives lc_at k (abs T (open_rec (S k) u body)). 
- true/false.

(9) `lc_open_back : forall k u t, lc_at k (open_rec k u t) -> lc_at (S k) t`. As argued. Proof induction t; bvar case with eqb; abs case IH at S k. Let's double check bvar case: t=bvar i. open_rec k u (bvar i) = if eqb k i then u else bvar i. If eqb true (i=k): hypothesis lc_at k u; conclusion lc_at (S k) (bvar k): k < S k. If false: hypothesis is lc_at k (bvar i) i.e., i<k; conclusion i<S k. Good.

(10) `step_lc : forall t t', lc_at 0 t -> t --> t' -> lc_at 0 t'`.
Induction on step derivation:
- ST_AppAbs: t = app (abs T body) v; lc_at 0 app → lc abs (lc_at 1 body) and lc v. Target lc_at 0 (open_rec 0 v body). Apply open_lc with k=0, u=v, t=body: need lc_at 0 v and lc_at 1 body. Good.
- ST_App1: app t1 t2 --> app t1' t2; lc gives lc t1, lc t2; IH gives lc t1'; lc_app.
- ST_App2: similarly.
We can do `induction 2` with IH; or intros; induction Hstep; inversion Hlc; subst; eauto using open_lc, lc_app. Need to be careful about naming.

(11) `step_det` and uniqueness. Let's define:
```
Lemma step_det : forall t t1 t2, t --> t1 -> t --> t2 -> t1 = t2.
```
Can we prove it directly? Induction on first derivation, then inversion on second. Cases:
- ST_AppAbs vs ST_AppAbs: same T,t,v? The first derivation: app (abs T t) v --> open t v with lc and value v. Second with T', t', v': app (abs T' t') v' --> open t' v'. Unification: abs T t = abs T' t' gives T=T', t=t'; v=v'. Result open t v = open t' v'. Good.
- ST_AppAbs vs ST_App1: second gives app (abs T t) v --> app t1' v where abs T t --> t1'; but abs is a value and value_no_step gives contradiction. Need value_no_step.
- ST_AppAbs vs ST_App2: second gives app (abs T t) v --> app (abs T t) v2' with value (abs T t) and v --> v2'; but v is a value (from first), contradiction via value_no_step.
- ST_App1 vs ST_AppAbs: first gives app t1 t2 with t1 --> t1'; second requires t1 = abs, but abs can't step; contradiction.
- ST_App1 vs ST_App1: t1 --> t1' and t1 --> t1''; IH gives t1'=t1''; result app t1' t2 = app t1'' t2.
- ST_App1 vs ST_App2: first gives t1 --> t1' (so t1 not a value); second requires value t1; contradiction via value_no_step? value t1 and t1 --> t1' contradiction. Yes.
- ST_App2 vs ST_AppAbs: first requires value v1 where t1 = v1; first: app v1 t2 --> app v1 t2' with t2 --> t2' and value v1. Second: app (abs T t) v --> open... so v1 = abs T t and t2 value... first has t2 --> t2', but value t2 → contradiction.
  Wait second ST_AppAbs requires value v, where v = t2. First has t2 --> t2'. value_no_step contradiction.
- ST_App2 vs ST_App1: first requires value t1; second requires t1 --> t1'; value_no_step contradiction.
- ST_App2 vs ST_App2: both v1 same? first: app v1 t2 --> app v1 t2', second: app v1' t2 --> app v1' t2''. Unify v1=v1'. IH on t2 steps gives t2'=t2''. Good.

This is provable but somewhat tedious with inversion. Alternatively, use the fact that `step` is deterministic syntactically; we might not need full determinism, only uniqueness in the specific expansion cases. Perhaps we can prove targeted lemmas:

For expansion at app abs v: given lc abs, value v. Show for any t'', app (abs T body) v --> t'' -> t'' = open body v. Cases on step: ST_AppAbs gives open body v (with possibly different body? unification ensures); ST_App1 needs abs --> t1' contradiction; ST_App2 needs body? no: value (abs) true and v --> v2' but v value → contradiction; also ST_App2 would give app abs v2'; contradiction via value_no_step. So targeted lemma easy.

For expansion at app t v where t --> t' uniquely: show app t v --> s -> s = app t' v. Cases: ST_AppAbs requires t = abs, but t --> t' contradicts value_no_step (abs value). ST_App1 gives t --> t1' so t1'=t' by uniqueness → s = app t' v. ST_App2 requires value t; contradiction with step/value_no_step. So targeted lemma also easy and avoids full det. But the expansion lemma is applied recursively at type T2 to `app t v` with t --> t' uniqueness; we need the uniqueness of `app t v`'s steps. So define generic lemma:

`app_step_unique : forall t t' v, t --> t' -> (forall t'', t --> t'' -> t'' = t') -> forall s, app t v --> s -> s = app t' v.`

But wait, to use ST_App1 on app t v, we need locally_closed v. In the expansion lemma, v is a value so lc. ST_App1's premise includes `locally_closed t2` where t2 = v. So if v not lc, ST_App1 can't apply; but then only ST_AppAbs/ST_App2 could; still resulting s = app t' v unlikely. Hmm, without lc v, app t v might be stuck, and s wouldn't exist... but the lemma says forall s, step -> s = app t' v; if no steps, vacuous. Fine. In the case ST_App1 applies we have lc v anyway from premise. So no issue.

So expansion lemma:
```
Lemma R_expand : forall T t t',
  lc_at 0 t ->
  t --> t' ->
  (forall t'', t --> t'' -> t'' = t') ->
  R T t' -> R T t.
```
Structural induction on T:
- Bool: split; lc assumption; sn via SN_intro: forall t'', t --> t'' -> sn t''; rewrite uniqueness → t'; R gives sn t'.
- Arrow T1 T2: split lc; split sn similarly; forall v Hval HR1, need R T2 (app t v). From HR' : R (Arrow T1 T2) t' get arrow property: R T2 (app t' v). Apply IH_R2 (structural IH for T2) with term (app t v), reduct (app t' v):
  - lc_at 0 (app t v): from lc t and value_lc v.
  - step: ST_App1 Hstep (value_lc v). Wait ST_App1's premise is `locally_closed t2`, i.e., lc_at 0 v. value_lc gives.
  - uniqueness: app_step_unique t t' v Hstep Huniq s H => s = app t' v.
  - R T2 (app t' v): from HR'.
  Then IH_R2 gives R T2 (app t v). Good.
Note: The structural induction hypothesis for T2 must be available. When doing `induction T`, in the arrow case T = Ty_Arrow T1 T2, the IH should include forall t t', ... for T1 and T2. We can write:
```
Lemma R_expand : forall T, (forall t t', lc_at 0 t -> t --> t' -> (forall t'', t --> t'' -> t'' = t') -> R T t' -> R T t)?? 
```
Better: `induction T as [|T1 IH1 T2 IH2]; intros t t' Hlc Hstep Huniq HR.` In arrow case, use IH2. Coq's induction on ty gives IH1, IH2 for the two types; we need the generalized statement. Let's write lemma with T first: `Lemma R_expand : forall T t t', ...`. Then `induction T; intros`. In the arrow case, IH for T1 and T2 are available as `IHT1 : forall t t', ... R T1 t' -> R T1 t` etc. Good.

(12) The semantic application lemma `R_app`. Let's work it out carefully with our R definition.

We want: forall T1 T2 t1 t2, R (T1 -> T2) t1 -> R T1 t2 -> R T2 (app t1 t2).

Unfold R arrow t1: get Hlc1, Hsn1, Harr: forall v, value v -> R T1 v -> R T2 (app t1 v).

We need to prove R T2 (app t1 t2) for arbitrary t2 with R T1 t2 (which gives lc t2, sn t2). Approach: outer well-founded induction on Hsn1, inner on Hsn2.

Actually maybe simpler: prove a generalized lemma by induction on the SN derivation of the *term* t1 with t2 generalized, and within App2 case use induction on SN of t2. But perhaps we can avoid the nested induction by using the "expansion" framework:

Observe: If t2 is a value, done via Harr. If t2 is not a value... but how do we get "not value" without decidability? We can case analyze on the *form* of t2. Values are abs/true/false. t2 can be bvar/fvar/app/abs/true/false. But a syntactically-abs t2 is not necessarily a value (needs lc: tm_abs T body value iff lc_at 0 (abs) which from R T1 t2 we have lc; so abs with lc IS a value via v_abs). t2 = true/false are values. t2 = bvar i: lc_at 0 t2 gives i<0 false → contradiction. t2 = fvar x: not a value; no step (fvars don't step). Then app t1 (fvar x): hmm, fvar x is stuck. We need R T2(app t1 (fvar x)). But fvar isn't a value and doesn't step, so app t1 (fvar x) doesn't reduce its argument, but can reduce t1 via App1. Since t1 SN, eventually t1 is a value and then app value (fvar x) is stuck (since AppAbs requires value argument, App2 requires value function plus argument steps; fvar doesn't step). So we should also induct on t1 to handle App1 cases.

Let's use the well-founded induction approach. Define the proposition:

`P(t1) := forall t2, R (T1->T2) t1 -> R T1 t2 -> R T2 (app t1 t2)`.

Prove P by induction on sn t1 (accessibility). But note R arrow t1 includes sn t1, and sn t1 must be the *same* accessibility derivation? We can get Hsn1 from R arrow; induction on it. In the induction step, IH: forall t1', t1 --> t1' -> P(t1')? Wait the induction principle with P depending on t1 only, t2 is bound inside P via forall. **Opening commutation details.**

We need a lemma: `open (subst sigma t) u = subst (update sigma x u) (open t (tm_fvar x))` for x fresh from t? Let's derive precisely.

Let `open t v := open_rec 0 v t`. We want:
`open_rec 0 (subst sigma u) (subst sigma t) = subst sigma' (open_rec 0 (tm_fvar x) t)` where sigma' = update sigma x u.

Using general lemma `subst_sigma_open`: 
`subst sigma' (open_rec k w t) = open_rec k (subst sigma' w) (subst sigma' t)` for all sigma', w, t. (Because both operations are structural and commute.)

Then RHS = open_rec 0 (subst sigma' (fvar x)) (subst sigma' t) = open_rec 0 u (subst sigma' t).

So we need `subst sigma' t = subst sigma t` when x is fresh for t. Actually sigma' = update sigma x u; if x not free in t, then subst sigma' t = subst sigma t. So LHS = open_rec 0 (subst sigma u) (subst sigma t). So indeed equality given freshness.

Alternatively, choose x fresh w.r.t. t (body) only; sigma can be arbitrary but update only differs at x.

Hmm wait, one more subtlety: In T_Abs case, the IH is for the typing judgment `update Gamma x T1 |-- open t1 (fvar x) : T2`, which we have. sem_type gives: forall sigma', env(update Gamma x T1) sigma' -> R T2 (subst sigma' (open t1 (fvar x))). For all y S, update Gamma x T1 y = Some S -> R S (update sigma x v y).

We need the equation `update Gamma x T1 y = Gamma y` for y ≠ x. `update Gamma x T1 y = if Nat.eqb x y then Some T1 else Gamma y`. If x ≠ y, Nat.eqb x y = false. Use `apply Nat.eqb_neq` or `Nat.eqb_eq`. We can destruct `Nat.eqb x y eqn:E`; `apply Nat.eqb_eq in E` gives x=y, contradiction.

Let's set up lemmas:
```
Lemma update_eq : forall Gamma x T, update Gamma x T x = Some T.
Proof. intros. unfold update. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. reflexivity. Qed.
Lemma update_neq : forall Gamma x y T, x <> y -> update Gamma x T y = Gamma y.
Proof. intros. unfold update. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E. apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. contradiction. reflexivity. Qed.
```
Nat.eqb_eq : forall n m, (n =? m) = true <-> n = m. In PeanoNat. Also Nat.eqb_neq: (n =? m) = false <-> n <> m.

**Freshness machinery.**

Define:
```
Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  end.
```

Fresh list function:
```
Definition fresh (L : list atom) : atom := S (fold_right Nat.max 0 L).
Lemma fresh_spec : forall L x, In x L -> x < fresh L.
Proof. intros L. induction L as [|a L IH]; intros x Hin; simpl in *; [contradiction|]. destruct Hin as [<-|Hin]; lia? 
```
We need max bound: fold_right Nat.max 0 L ≥ each element. Prove `forall L x, In x L -> x <= fold_right Nat.max 0 L`. By induction:
- x = a: a <= max a (fold...) since max a b >= a. Use Nat.le_max_l.
- x in L: IH gives x <= fold L <= max a (fold L) = fold (a::L). Use Nat.le_max_r and trans.
Then x < S (fold ...). Use lia.

Actually simpler: define fresh as `S (fold_right Nat.max 0 L)`. We need `~ In (fresh L) L` from fresh_spec: if In then fresh L < fresh L, contradiction. Good.

Note: `fresh L` is an atom (nat). Good.

But careful: fold_right Nat.max 0 L — Nat.max notation? In PeanoNat, `Nat.max`. We'll use `Nat.max`. Also can use `List.fold_right`.

There may be a simpler existing library function: `List.list_max`? Hmm, `List.fold_right Nat.max 0`. Fine.

**Substitution lemmas.**

```
Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm := ...
```

Lemmas:
- `subst_fvar : subst sigma (tm_fvar x) = sigma x` (by simpl).
- `subst_open` : forall sigma k u t, subst sigma (open_rec k u t) = open_rec k (subst sigma u) (subst sigma t). Induction on t.
- `subst_lc`: forall k t sigma, lc_at k t -> (forall x, lc_at 0 (sigma x)) -> lc_at k (subst sigma t). Wait under binders k increases but sigma terms are lc_at 0 and lc_at 0 implies lc_at k for any k ≥ 0 via monotonicity. Proof: induction on t:
  - bvar: subst = bvar i; lc_bvar.
  - fvar: sigma x, lc_at 0; need lc_at k (sigma x): monotonicity from 0 ≤ k.
  - app: IH.
  - abs: hypothesis lc_at k (abs T body) gives lc_at (S k) body; IH with k:=S k gives lc_at (S k) (subst sigma body); lc_abs.
  - true/false.
We need monotonicity lemma `lc_at_mono`.

- `fv_subst`? We need `subst sigma' t = subst sigma t` when sigma' = update sigma x v and x ∉ fv t. Prove a general lemma:
```
Lemma fv_correct : forall x t, In x (fv t) <-> free_in x t  -- maybe not needed.

**Typing → lc.**

Lemma typing_lc : forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T -> lc_at 0 t.

**Now the fundamental theorem.**

```
Definition sem_type (Gamma : context) (t : tm) (T : ty) : Prop :=
  forall sigma,
    (forall x S, Gamma x = Some S -> R S (sigma x)) ->
    R T (subst sigma t).
```

Prove:
```
Lemma fundamental : forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T -> sem_type Gamma t T.
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T H. induction H.
```
Let's think about each constructor.

- T_Var: Goal sem_type Gamma (fvar x) T, i.e., forall sigma, env -> R T (subst sigma (fvar x)) = R T (sigma x). Intro sigma Henv. apply Henv. assumption. (Note Gamma x = Some T from constructor.)
- T_App: Goal sem_type Gamma (app t1 t2) T2. intros sigma Henv. simpl subst: subst sigma (app t1 t2) = app (subst sigma t1)(subst sigma t2). IH1: sem_type Gamma t1 (Arrow T1 T2) — wait the constructor's premises: has_type Gamma t1 (T1 -> T2) and has_type Gamma t2 T1. IH1 applied to sigma Henv gives R (T1->T2) (subst sigma t1); IH2 gives R T1 (subst sigma t2). Apply R_app. Good.
- T_True/T_False: need R Bool (subst sigma true) = R Bool true = lc_at 0 true /\ sn true. simpa using lc_true, sn_true.
- T_Abs: Goal sem_type Gamma (abs T1 t1) (T1 -> T2). intros sigma Henv. Need R (Arrow T1 T2) (subst sigma (tm_abs T1 t1)) = R (Arrow T1 T2)(tm_abs T1 (subst sigma t1)).
  Components:
  (a) lc_at 0 (tm_abs T1 (subst sigma t1)): i.e., lc_at 1 (subst sigma t1). From typing_lc on the T_Abs derivation → lc_at 0 (abs T1 t1) → lc_at 1 t1; subst_lc with env lc (forall x, lc_at 0 (sigma x)) — derive from Henv and R_lc. Good.
  (b) sn (tm_abs T1 (subst sigma t1)): sn_abs.
  (c) forall v, value v -> R T1 v -> R T2 (app (tm_abs T1 (subst sigma t1)) v).
      intros v Hv HRv.
      We want R T2(app (abs T1 body') v). Strategy:
        - Get IH_open: sem_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2 for fresh x (fresh w.r.t. L and fv t1).
        - Instantiate sigma' := update sigma x v, env: R S (sigma' y).
        - Get R T2 (subst sigma' (open t1 (fvar x))).
        - Show subst sigma' (open t1 x) = open (subst sigma t1) v. (commutation + freshness)
        - So R T2 (open body' v).
        - Use R_expand at type T2 with t = app (abs T1 body') v, t' = open body' v:
            * lc_at 0 t: lc_at 0 (abs) (from (a)) and value_lc v.
            * step: ST_AppAbs with locally_closed (abs) := lc_at 0 (abs) and Hv. Note step constructor takes `locally_closed (tm_abs T t)` and `value v`.
            * uniqueness: any step of app (abs T1 body') v yields open body' v. Prove lemma `step_abs_app_unique`.
            * R T2 (open body' v) done.
        - Conclude R T2(app abs v).
  
  Wait, do we need R_expand at T2 to be available? It's proven before. Good.

  But hold on: the IH from induction on has_type for T_Abs: `IHhas_type : forall x, ~ In x L -> sem_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2`. Yes if we structured properly.

  Freshness: choose x := fresh (L ++ fv t1). Need ~In x L and ~In x (fv t1): from ~In x (L ++ fv t1) and In_app_iff. We also need x fresh for... the step's lc? No. sigma' update at x is the same x used in open t1 x. Good.

  But wait: we also need `x` fresh w.r.t. `sigma`? No.

  Let's verify the commutation in detail:
  `subst sigma' (open t1 (fvar x))` where `open t1 u = open_rec 0 u t1`. 
  By `subst_open` (subst commute with open_rec): subst sigma' (open_rec 0 (fvar x) t1) = open_rec 0 (subst sigma' (fvar x)) (subst sigma' t1) = open_rec 0 v (subst sigma' t1).
  Since x ∉ fv t1, subst sigma' t1 = subst sigma t1 (subst_ext).
  So = open_rec 0 v (subst sigma t1) = open (subst sigma t1) v = open body' v. 
  
  Careful: the notation `open` defined as `open t u := open_rec 0 u t` (t first, u second). So `open body' v = open_rec 0 v body'`. Yes.

  The expansion target: `app (tm_abs T1 body') v` reduces to `open body' v = open_rec 0 v body'`. ST_AppAbs definition: `tm_app (tm_abs T t) v --> open t v` where `open t v = open_rec 0 v t`. With body' as t. Yes.

**R_expand and R_app details.**

Let's write R_expand:

```
Lemma R_expand : forall T t t',
  lc_at 0 t ->
  step t t' ->
  (forall t'', step t t'' -> t'' = t') ->
  R T t' ->
  R T t.
Proof.
  induction T as [| T1 IH1 T2 IH2]; intros t t' Hlc Hstep Huniq HR.
  - (* Bool *) simpl in *. destruct HR as [Hlc' Hsn']. split; [exact Hlc|].
    apply SN_intro. intros t'' Hst. rewrite (Huniq t'' Hst). exact Hsn'.
  - (* Arrow *) simpl in *. destruct HR as [Hlc' [Hsn' Harr]]. split; [exact Hlc|]. split.
    + apply SN_intro. intros t'' Hst. rewrite (Huniq t'' Hst). exact Hsn'.
    + intros v Hv HRv.
      apply (IH2 (tm_app t v) (tm_app t' v)).
      * apply lc_app; [exact Hlc | apply value_lc; exact Hv].
      * apply ST_App1; [exact Hstep | apply value_lc; exact Hv].
      * intros s Hst. apply app_step_unique_general; auto.
      * apply Harr; [exact Hv | exact HRv].
Qed.
```

Wait, ST_App1's second premise is `locally_closed t2` where t2 = v; `locally_closed v` unfolds to lc_at 0 v. value_lc gives.

The uniqueness for app t v: `forall s, step (app t v) s -> s = app t' v`. Prove a lemma:

```
Lemma step_app1_unique : forall t t' v,
  step t t' ->
  (forall t'', step t t'' -> t'' = t') ->
  forall s, step (tm_app t v) s -> s = tm_app t' v.
Proof.
  intros t t' v Hstep Huniq s Hs.
  inversion Hs; subst; clear Hs.
  - (* ST_AppAbs: t = tm_abs T t0, v = v0, s = open t0 v0 *) 
     (* but then tm_abs T t0 has a step t' via Hstep, contradicting value_no_step *)
     exfalso. eapply value_no_step; [apply v_abs; eassumption | exact Hstep]. 
     Hmm: need locally_closed (tm_abs T t0); ST_AppAbs gives it; and value v. 
  - (* ST_App1: t --> t1', s = app t1' v *) 
     rewrite (Huniq t1' H1). reflexivity. Need identify H1 : step t t1'.
  - (* ST_App2: value t, t2 --> t2' *) 
     exfalso. eapply value_no_step; [exact Hvalue | exact Hstep].
Qed.
```

Let me be careful with inversion naming. We'll iterate in Coq.

Similarly `step_abs_app_unique`:
```
Lemma step_abs_app_unique : forall T body v s,
  lc_at 0 (tm_abs T body) ->
  value v ->
  step (tm_app (tm_abs T body) v) s ->
  s = open body v.
Proof.
  intros. inversion H1; subst.
  - reflexivity? The ST_AppAbs case gives s = open t v (with t=body, v=v). Need unify parameters: conclusion `tm_app (tm_abs T0 t) v0 = tm_app (tm_abs T body) v`; inversion gives T0=T, t=body, v0=v; s = open body v. reflexivity.
  - (* ST_App1: need step (tm_abs T body) t1' *) exfalso; eapply value_no_step; [apply v_abs; exact H | exact Hstep].
  - (* ST_App2: value (tm_abs T body) and step v t2' *) exfalso; eapply value_no_step; [exact Hvalue | exact Hstep].
Qed.
```
Wait, in ST_App1 case, step (tm_abs T body) t1' arises; use value_no_step with v_abs: need locally_closed (abs); we have H. In ST_App2, step v t2' contradicts value v.

Actually maybe we can prove `value_no_step` such that it works with any value; in ST_App2 the value is v : value v (given).

**R_app proof details.**

```
Lemma R_app : forall T1 T2 t1 t2,

Proof.
  intros T1 T2 t1 t2 HR1 HR2.

  revert Harr t2 HR2.

```
  intros Harr t2 HR2.
  pose proof (R_sn _ _ HR2) as Hsn2.

The names in contexts: Hred : forall t1', step t1 t1' -> sn t1'? Actually the induction principle of strongly_normalizing: constructor SN_intro t (forall t', step t t' -> sn t'). `induction Hsn1 as [t1 Hred IH]` gives Hred : forall t1', step t1 t1' -> sn t1' (the actual premise) and IH : forall t1', step t1 t1' -> (forall Harr t2, R T1 t2 -> R T2 (app t1' t2))? Careful: Coq's induction principle for `strongly_normalizing`:

```
strongly_normalizing_ind :
forall P : tm -> Prop,
  (forall t, (forall t', step t t' -> strongly_normalizing t') ->
            (forall t', step t t' -> P t') -> P t) ->
  forall t, strongly_normalizing t -> P t
```

Actually, let's step back and think of a classical proof of SN for CBV STLC using **reducibility candidates where R_Arrow is defined on values only, and separately C_T is closure under "evaluation"**:

Define:
- `V_Bool(v) := v = true \/ v = false` (for values).
- `V_Arrow(v) := exists T1 T2 body, v = abs T1 body /\ forall u, C_T1(u) -> C_T2(app v u)`.

Then prove:
1. C_T ⊆ SN (by def).
2. Values that are V are also C? If v is a value, sn v trivially; forall t', v -->* t' -> value t' -> ... multistep from value: only reflexivity, so t' = v; need V_T(v). So `V_T v -> C_T v`.
3. Closure of C under expansion? If t --> t' and C_T(t'), then C_T(t)? sn: t' SN + unique reduct; but t may have other reducts? Determinism. For the forall t' part: multistep t -->* s value implies t' -->* s (determinism); V_T(s) from C_T(t'). Hmm, actually if t --> t'' and t'' ≠ t', then determinism fails; need determinism.
4. Fundamental theorem: Gamma |-- t : T and env sigma with C_{Gamma x}(sigma x) -> C_T(subst sigma t).
   - T_Abs: need C_{T1->T2}(abs body'). SN of abs (trivial). For any multistep from abs to a value: abs is a value, so only itself; need V_Arrow(abs body'): forall u, C_T1 u -> C_T2(app abs u). We know from IH: for all sigma' env, C_T2(subst sigma' (open body x)). For u in C_T1: need C_T2(app abs u). app abs u --> open body u (needs lc abs; from typing→lc + subst lc + u lc?). Does C_T1 u give lc? Hmm C doesn't include lc. But V/C for typed closed terms... We can include lc in C: C_T(t) := lc_at 0 t /\ sn t /\ ... Then env gives lc u. Then app abs u is a term that is not a value (it's an app) reducing in one step to open body u. C_T2(app abs u) requires sn(app abs u) and all reachable values in V. From C_T2(open body u), and app abs u --> open body u with unique reduct? Need determinism again to conclude sn and multistep behavior. Essentially the same issues.

Lemma R_stuck : forall t, lc_at 0 t -> (forall s, ~ step t s) -> (forall T, R T t).

This suggests defining a Boolean/option step function `step_fun : tm -> option tm` and proving:
- `step_fun_spec : forall t t', step t t' <-> step_fun t = Some t'`.
Since step is deterministic, this is clean! Define:
```
Fixpoint step_fun (t : tm) : option tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ | tm_fvar _ | tm_true | tm_false | tm_abs _ _ => None
  | tm_app t1 t2 =>
      match t1 with
      | tm_abs T body =>
          if value_dec? t2 then Some (open body t2)
          else match step_fun t2 with Some t2' => Some (app t1 t2') | None => None end
      | _ =>
          match step_fun t1 with
          | Some t1' => if lc_dec t2 then Some (app t1' t2) else None
          | None =>
              match ... if value_dec t1 then match step_fun t2 with ... 
          end
      end
  end
```
This requires decidable `value` and `lc_at`, which require decidable lc_at (structural, with Nat comparisons) and value (value v for abs requires lc_at 0 abs which is decidable structurally!). lc_at is a Prop but decidable by recursion. Actually lc_at k t is decidable by structural recursion on t. value t: abs requires lc, true/false. Decidable.

However, wait: do we even need "closed fvar-free" syntax? In R_app, t1 and t2 are in R, which includes closed (lc+nofvar). **Another classic approach: Normalization by evaluation / Kripke logical relation with values.** Let me think about the "value" relation approach more carefully, defining:

Then the fundamental theorem: C_T components; application case: t1 ∈ C_(A→B), t2 ∈ C_A. We know t1 -->* v1 (value, V_Arrow), t2 -->* v2 (value, V_A). Define:
- `halts_v (t : tm) (T : ty) : Prop` maybe mutually with V.

Hmm, but V_Arrow(v) references C_T1/C_T2, which reference V_T1/V_T2 structurally smaller: mutual recursion on ty. We can define by Fixpoint on T returning a pair of predicates? In Coq, mutual Fixpoint on ty:
```
Fixpoint V (T : ty) (t : tm) {struct T} : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Bool => t = tm_true \/ t = tm_false
  | Arrow T1 T2 => closed t /\ forall u, C T1 u -> C T2 (app t u)
  end
with C (T : ty) (t : tm) {struct T} : Prop :=
  closed t /\ sn t /\ exists v, multi step t v /\ value v /\ V T v.
```
Mutual fixpoints on ty with C and V having same structural argument; in the Arrow case, V calls C T1 and C T2, and C T2 refers to V T2. Should be accepted (guard condition: recursive calls on T1/T2 subterms). C calls V T (same T) and V calls C on smaller types; mutual recursion where each call decreases or stays? The guard checker requires recursive calls in a mutual block to be on structurally smaller arguments? Actually for mutual fixpoints, calls between functions in the block must be on the same or smaller argument? The guard condition: recursive calls must be on structurally smaller arguments of the same function or mutual partner? I recall mutual Fixpoints require each recursive call to have a structural argument that is a subterm of the corresponding function's decreasing argument, and calls to the partner must decrease relative to the partner's parameter? Let's test. C T calls V T — same argument, not smaller. The guard checker for mutual recursion: it uses a "recursive call" check where the decreasing argument must be a subterm of the original for the called function's parameter. C T -> V T is not smaller, likely rejected ("recursive call to V has principal argument equal to T instead of a subterm"). Hmm.

We can avoid mutual recursion by defining V and C in one function returning a record/pair:
```
Fixpoint RV (T : ty) : (tm -> Prop) * (tm -> Prop) :=
  match T with
  | Bool => (fun t => t = true \/ t = false, fun t => closed t /\ sn t /\ exists v, multi step t v /\ value v /\ (v = true \/ v = false))
  | Arrow T1 T2 =>
      let (V1,C1) := RV T1 in
      let (V2,C2) := RV T2 in
      (fun t => closed t /\ forall u, C1 u -> C2 (app t u),
       fun t => closed t /\ sn t /\ exists v, multi step t v /\ value v /\ closed v /\ forall u, C1 u -> C2 (app v u))
  end.
```
This is a structural Fixpoint on T with `let` destructuring of recursive calls on subterms T1, T2. Coq should accept (recursive calls on strict subterms). We can define projections V T and C T.

But hold on: C_T includes `exists v, t -->* v /\ value v /\ V_T(v)`. To prove C_T for constructed terms, we need to exhibit multi-step reductions. For t1 ∈ C_A→B we get a value v1 with t1 -->* v1 and V_Arrow(v1). For t2 likewise. Then t1 t2 -->* v1 t2 (App1 congruence) and v1 t2 -->* v1 v2 (App2 congruence), so t1 t2 -->* v1 v2. Then ST_AppAbs: v1 v2 --> open body v2. So t1 t2 -->* open body v2. Need C_B(open body v2): this requires sn (from...) and existence of a value w with open body v2 -->* w and V_B(w). Hmm, we'd need to know open body v2 halts — that's not given by V_Arrow(v1) unless V_Arrow gives C_B(v1 v2). Wait V_Arrow(v1) := closed v1 /\ forall u, C_A(u) -> C_B(app v1 u). From C_B(app v1 t2): value w with app v1 t2 -->* w. And t1 t2 -->* app v1 t2. So t1 t2 -->* w. Also sn(t1 t2): need follow from sn(t1), sn(t2), and... Since app v1 t2 is SN and t1 t2 -->* app v1 t2, does that make t1 t2 SN? The path from t1 t2 to app v1 t2 is finite and unique, and then SN; so yes, using determinism and a lemma "if t -->* u and sn u then sn t" (MULTI-step backward preservation). We can prove by induction on multistep using: if t --> t1 and sn t1 then sn t (needs determinism: all reducts of t equal t1). So yes.

So the C/V approach reduces the hard part to proving determinism and multistep congruence, but avoids the awkward R_app arrow-property issue since application is only required at values (abs), where beta works. Let's examine:

So the C/V approach needs:

**Definition attempt:**

```
Definition closed_tm (t : tm) : Prop := lc_at 0 t.
```
(no nofvar needed). Does C need lc? Env must give lc for subst_lc. C's closed component gives lc for sigma x. Let's include `lc_at 0 t` in C and V.

```
Fixpoint V (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => lc_at 0 t /\ (t = tm_true \/ t = tm_false)
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      lc_at 0 t /\ (forall u, C T1 u -> C T2 (tm_app t u))

with C (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  lc_at 0 t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\

```

Mutual recursion issue: V T calls C T1, C T2 (smaller types); C T calls V T (same type). Mutual Fixpoint guard might reject the same-type call. Let's avoid mutual recursion by defining C in terms of V, and V in terms of C, but perhaps define a single function returning both predicates as a pair:

```
Fixpoint VC (T : ty) : (tm -> Prop) * (tm -> Prop) :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool =>
      (fun t => lc_at 0 t /\ (t = tm_true \/ t = tm_false),
       fun t => lc_at 0 t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
                 (exists v, multi step t v /\ value v /\
                    (lc_at 0 v /\ (v = tm_true \/ v = tm_false))))
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      let (V1, C1) := VC T1 in
      let (V2, C2) := VC T2 in
      (fun t => lc_at 0 t /\ (forall u, C1 u -> C2 (tm_app t u)),
       fun t => lc_at 0 t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
                 (exists v, multi step t v /\ value v /\ V2?? ))
  end.
```
In the arrow case, C needs V of the arrow type itself, which is defined by the pair being built. We can write:
```
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      let p1 := VC T1 in
      let p2 := VC T2 in
      let V1 := fst p1 in let C1 := snd p1 in
      let V2 := fst p2 in let C2 := snd p2 in
      let Varr := (fun t => lc_at 0 t /\ (forall u, C1 u -> C2 (app t u))) in
      (Varr,
       fun t => lc_at 0 t /\ sn t /\ (exists v, multi step t v /\ value v /\ Varr v))
```
Here C's definition uses Varr directly (a local let), not a recursive call. But this definition is a Fixpoint on T with recursive calls VC T1, VC T2. The body of the arrow case calls VC T1 and VC T2 on subterms — accepted. However, note Varr is built from C1, C2 which are projections of p1, p2. Coq accepts lets around recursive calls? The guard checker should allow recursive calls in let bindings. I think so, but `let (V1,C1) := VC T1` might complicate. Alternative: define using explicit projections:

```
Fixpoint CV (T : ty) : (tm -> Prop) * (tm -> Prop) :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => (fun t => lc_at 0 t /\ (t = tm_true \/ t = tm_false),
                fun t => lc_at 0 t /\ sn t /\ (exists v, multi step t v /\ value v /\
                          (lc_at 0 v /\ (v = tm_true \/ v = tm_false))))
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      (fun t => lc_at 0 t /\ (forall u, (snd (CV T1)) u -> (snd (CV T2)) (tm_app t u)),
       fun t => lc_at 0 t /\ sn t /\
                (exists v, multi step t v /\ value v /\ (fst (CV (Ty_Arrow T1 T2))) v))
  end.
```
Oops, the C case calls CV (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) — same argument as the outer, rejected. But we can avoid by substituting the definition of Varr locally. The problem is the recursive occurrence at the same type. Use a helper definition for Varr inside:

```
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      let C1 := snd (CV T1) in
      let C2 := snd (CV T2) in
      let Varr := fun t => lc_at 0 t /\ (forall u, C1 u -> C2 (tm_app t u)) in
      (Varr,
       fun t => lc_at 0 t /\ sn t /\ (exists v, multi step t v /\ value v /\ Varr v))
```
Recursive calls CV T1, CV T2 only on subterms. Then define:
```
Definition V (T : ty) : tm -> Prop := fst (CV T).
Definition C (T : ty) : tm -> Prop := snd (CV T).
```
Then when reasoning, we unfold CV at Bool/Arrow. Need lemmas:
```
Lemma V_bool : forall t, V Ty_Bool t <-> lc_at 0 t /\ (t = true \/ t = false).
Lemma V_arrow : forall T1 T2 t, V (Arrow T1 T2) t <-> lc_at 0 t /\ (forall u, C T1 u -> C T2 (app t u)).

Lemma C_arrow : forall T1 T2 t, C (Arrow T1 T2) t <-> lc_at 0 t /\ sn t /\ (exists v, multi step t v /\ value v /\ V (Arrow T1 T2) v).
```
These follow by `simpl`/`reflexivity`? Because CV is a Fixpoint; `V (Arrow T1 T2) t` unfolds to `fst (CV (Arrow T1 T2)) t` then `let C1 := snd (CV T1) ...` — need `cbn`. Should work. But careful: Coq's reduction of `CV (Arrow T1 T2)` may not reduce if T1/T2 are variables? Fixpoint on T = Ty_Arrow T1 T2 reduces since constructor. Yes.

When we later unfold V (Arrow T1 T2) t, C1 = snd (CV T1) = C T1 definitionally, C2 = C T2. So `simpl` gives the expected. Good.

**Now prove V/C basic properties.**

(1) `V_lc`, `C_lc`.
(2) `V_sn?` Is a value in V necessarily SN? V_Bool: t = true/false → sn. V_Arrow: abs? Wait V_Arrow(t) doesn't require t to be a value! V_Arrow(t) = lc t /\ forall u, C T1 u -> C T2(app t u). Wait C T v unfolds to: lc v /\ sn v /\ exists v', multi step v v' /\ value v' /\ V T v'. Take v'=v. Good.

(3) `C_sn`, `C_lc` projections.

(4) `C_multi_value`? C T t gives exists v, t -->* v /\ value v /\ V T v. Good.

(5) `V_Bool` values: if V Bool t then t = true or t = false, so it's a value and sn.

(6) Key: `C_arrow_value_apply`? From C (Arrow T1 T2) t, get v with t -->* v, value v, V_Arrow(v), so forall u, C T1 u -> C T2(app v u). Good.

(7) `V_value_C` as above.

(8) `C_value`: for a value v with V T v, C T v. (same as V_value_C).

**Step/multistep lemmas.**

- `step_det`.
- `sn_step` forward: sn t -> step t t' -> sn t'.
- `sn_back_step`: step t t' -> sn t' -> sn t (uses step_det).
- `sn_multi_back`: multi step t t' -> sn t' -> sn t (induction on multi using sn_back_step). Note multi is defined with constructor multi_step x y z: R x y -> multi R y z -> multi R x z. So the first step is t --> y and y -->* z.
- `multi_trans` maybe needed? Not if we compose via constructors.
- `multi_app1`: multi step t1 t1' -> lc_at 0 t2 -> multi step (app t1 t2) (app t1' t2).
  Induction on multi: 
  - refl: multi_refl.
  - multi_step t1 y z Hstep Hmulti: need multi (app t1 t2) (app z t2). Use multi_step with R step: step (app t1 t2) (app y t2) via ST_App1 Hstep Hlc, then IH Hmulti.
- `multi_app2`: value v1 -> multi step t2 t2' -> multi step (app v1 t2) (app v1 t2').
  Similarly using ST_App2 Hval.
- Also `multi_app_abs`: maybe combine: app abs body (…) 
- `step_abs`: lc abs -> value v -> step (app abs v) (open body v) via ST_AppAbs.

**Backward SN along multistep**: `sn_multi_back : forall t t', multi step t t' -> sn t' -> sn t`. Proof induction on multi:
- refl t: sn t' = sn t assumption.
- multi_step t y z Hst Hm IH: We have IH? `induction Hm`? Let's set: `intros t t' Hm. induction Hm as [x | x y z Hstep Hm IH]; intros Hsn.` 
  - refl: exact Hsn.
  - step case: Hm : multi step y z; IH : sn z -> sn y; goal sn x given Hsn : sn z. Apply sn_back_step with y: need sn y, from IH Hsn. Then sn_back_step Hstep (IH Hsn). Good.

`sn_back_step`: step t t' -> sn t' -> sn t. Proof: intros Hstep Hsn. constructor. intros t'' Hstep''. assert (t'' = t') by (eapply step_det; eauto); subst; exact Hsn. Wait SN_intro's hypothesis is forall t', step t t' -> sn t'. After constructor, goal is `forall t'', step t t'' -> sn t''`. Using step_det Hstep'' Hstep gives t'' = t'. Then rewrite. Good.

**Opening/typing/substitution lemmas.**

- open_lc (forward), lc_open_back.
- subst and subst_open, subst_ext, subst_lc.
- typing_lc.

**Fundamental theorem.**

Statement:
```
Definition env_ok (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  forall x S, Gamma x = Some S -> C S (sigma x).
```
Then `sem_type Gamma t T := forall sigma, env_ok Gamma sigma -> C T (subst sigma t)`.

Prove has_type -> sem_type by induction.

- T_Var: env gives C T (sigma x); subst sigma (fvar x) = sigma x.
- T_App: IH1 gives C (T1->T2) (subst sigma t1), IH2 gives C T1 (subst sigma t2). Need C T2 (app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)). Use lemma `C_app : forall T1 T2 t1 t2, C (Arrow T1 T2) t1 -> C T1 t2 -> C T2 (app t1 t2)`.
- T_Abs: Need C (Arrow T1 T2) (tm_abs T1 (subst sigma t1)).
  Components:
  - lc: lc_at 1 (subst sigma t1) from typing_lc (lc_at 1 t1) and subst_lc with env lc (C_lc from env). 
  - sn: sn of abs — unconditional `sn_abs`.
  - exists v: take v = abs T1 (subst sigma t1); multi_refl; value v needs lc_at 0 abs — we have lc; V (Arrow T1 T2) v = lc v /\ forall u, C T1 u -> C T2(app v u).
    For u with C T1 u: need C T2 (app (abs T1 body') u).
    Choose fresh x ∉ L ∪ fv t1. Get IH_open := IH at x (from T_Abs premise), i.e., sem_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2.
    C T1 u gives a value w with u -->* w, value w, V T1 w. Then V T1 w -> C T1 w (V_value_C). Apply IH_open with sigma' = update sigma x w:
       env_ok (update Gamma x T1) sigma': for y:
         - y = x: update Gamma x T1 x = Some T1; need C T1 (update sigma x w x) = C T1 w. Have.
         - y ≠ x: update Gamma x T1 y = Gamma y; update sigma x w y = sigma y; env Gamma sigma gives C S (sigma y).
       gives C T2 (subst sigma' (open t1 (fvar x))).
       By commutation and freshness: subst sigma' (open t1 (fvar x)) = open (subst sigma t1) w = open body' w.
       So C T2 (open body' w).
    Now need C T2 (app abs u):
       closed: lc abs (have) and lc u (C T1 u → C_lc).
       sn: app abs u -->* app abs w (multi_app2 with value abs, multi u w) --> open body' w (ST_AppAbs with lc abs, value w). Compose multistep. C T2(open body' w) gives sn(open body' w). Then sn_multi_back gives sn(app abs u).
       exists value: C T2(open body' w) gives z with open body' w -->* z, value z, V T2 z. Compose multistep app abs u -->* open body' w -->* z. 
       So C T2(app abs u). 
  - Therefore C (Arrow T1 T2)(abs body'). 

Wait: for C's exists component, we need `multi step t v` where t = abs body' and v = abs body'. multi_refl works. value v: v_abs requires locally_closed (abs) = lc_at 0 abs. Good.

But careful: `C (Arrow T1 T2) (subst sigma (abs ...))`: subst sigma (tm_abs T1 t1) = tm_abs T1 (subst sigma t1). So the term is abs. Good.

**Lemma C_app** (the T_App case):
Given C (Arrow T1 T2) t1 and C T1 t2.
- C t1 gives lc, sn, and v1 with t1 -->* v1, value v1, V_Arrow v1.
- Need C T2 (app t1 t2):
  - lc: lc_app.
  - sn: V_Arrow v1 gives forall u, C T1 u -> C T2(app v1 u). Apply with u = t2: C T2(app v1 t2), so sn(app v1 t2). And app t1 t2 -->* app v1 t2 via multi_app1 Hmulti1 (C_lc t2). sn_multi_back gives sn(app t1 t2).
  - exists w with app t1 t2 -->* w, value w, V T2 w: from C T2(app v1 t2), get w with app v1 t2 -->* w, value w, V T2 w. Compose multi: app t1 t2 -->* app v1 t2 -->* w. Need multi_trans (or construct by induction). Define `multi_trans` lemma. Actually multi's constructors make composition easy: `multi_step ...`? If we have H1 : multi step a b and H2 : multi step b c, we can prove multi step a c by induction on H1. Need a lemma `multi_trans`.
  Alternatively, prove `multi_app1` and then compose: multi_trans H1 H2.

So need `multi_trans : forall X (R : relation X) a b c, multi R a b -> multi R b c -> multi R a c`.

**Does C_app need V_Arrow v1's `lc_at 0 v1`?** V includes lc; fine.

**Details on the fresh x in T_Abs.**

The T_Abs constructor: `T_Abs : forall (L : list atom) Gamma T1 T2 t1, (forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2) -> has_type Gamma (tm_abs T1 t1) (T1 -> T2)`.

Induction: `induction H` for H : has_type Gamma t T. For T_Abs case, the IH is `IHhas_type : forall x : atom, ~ In x L -> sem_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2`? Actually induction on H with the goal `sem_type Gamma t T`; in the T_Abs case, t = tm_abs T1 t1, T = T1->T2. The IH corresponds to the premise: `forall x, ~ In x L -> sem_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2`. Coq's induction should produce this if the recursive occurrence is under the binder. Let's verify: The has_type_ind principle for this inductive:
```
has_type_ind : forall P : context -> tm -> ty -> Prop,
  (forall Gamma x T, Gamma x = Some T -> P Gamma (tm_fvar x) T) ->
  (forall L Gamma T1 T2 t1,
      (forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2) ->
      (forall x, ~ In x L -> P (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2) ->
      P Gamma (tm_abs T1 t1) (T1 -> T2)) ->
  ...
```
Yes, standard.

So in T_Abs case: IH : forall x, ~ In x L -> sem_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2. We choose x := fresh (L ++ fv t1). Get HxL : ~ In x L and Hxfv : ~ In x (fv t1).

We need `fv` and `fresh` lemmas.

Alternatively, can we avoid fv/fresh by choosing x from L somehow? L could contain all needed? We need x ∉ L. And x ∉ fv t1 for commutation. Since we can choose arbitrarily fresh, use `fresh (L ++ fv t1)`.

**Commutation lemma revisited**:
`subst sigma' (open t1 (tm_fvar x)) = open (subst sigma t1) w` where sigma' = update sigma x w and x ∉ fv t1.
- subst_open_comm: subst sigma' (open_rec 0 (fvar x) t1) = open_rec 0 (subst sigma' (fvar x)) (subst sigma' t1).
- subst sigma' (fvar x) = sigma' x = w.
- subst sigma' t1 = subst sigma t1 by subst_ext (x ∉ fv t1).
Good.

**Environment update reasoning**: need `update` lemmas and function extensionality? We compare pointwise; no funext needed except maybe `update Gamma x T1 = ...` no.

**Step determinism.**

Let's write carefully. Notation `t --> t'` is step. We'll write it explicitly in lemma statements to avoid notation issues? The file has notation available. We can use `step`.

```
Lemma step_det : forall t t1 t2, step t t1 -> step t t2 -> t1 = t2.
Proof.
  intros t t1 t2 H1. revert t2. induction H1; intros t2 H2.
  - (* ST_AppAbs T t v Hlc Hv *) inversion H2; subst.
    + reflexivity.
    + (* ST_App1: step (tm_abs T t) t1' *) exfalso. eapply value_no_step; [apply v_abs; exact Hlc | exact H].
      Need names. Let's see constructor argument ordering: ST_AppAbs : forall T t v, locally_closed (tm_abs T t) -> value v -> step (tm_app (tm_abs T t) v) (open t v).
      ST_App1 : forall t1 t1' t2, step t1 t1' -> locally_closed t2 -> step (tm_app t1 t2) (tm_app t1' t2).
      ST_App2 : forall v1 t2 t2', value v1 -> step t2 t2' -> step (tm_app v1 t2) (tm_app v1 t2').
      Inversion on H2 for each H1 case.
  ...
```
This may be finicky. Maybe there's a smarter proof using inversion on both and automation. Let's write it manually later in Coq and iterate.

Alternatively, can we avoid determinism? Let's see where it's needed in the C/V approach:
- sn_back_step / sn_multi_back. Needed for sn of composed reductions.
Is there another way to get sn(app t1 t2) without backward transfer? In C_app, we know C_T2(app v1 t2) gives sn(app v1 t2), and app t1 t2 -->* app v1 t2. Without determinism, t1 could have infinitely many other paths; but typed t1 ∈ C and C values... Hmm, we don't have SN of t1? C gives sn t1. Actually for sn(app t1 t2), if sn t1 and t1 -->* v1, does that help? SN t1 means all paths from t1 finite; app t1 t2 steps when t1 steps, giving app t1' t2; those are finite, but app v1 t2 path needs sn. Since t1 SN and deterministic? Without determinism, t1 might have multiple reducts. But all reducts are SN, so no infinite path from t1; however app t1 t2 could still diverge through App1 steps only if t1 diverges. Since t1 SN, App1 steps terminate; then at value v? t1's normal form might be a value v1'; C gives a value reachable, but if nondeterministic, t1 could also reach a stuck normal form. Then app stuck → fine. Hmm, proving this without determinism is complicated but probably doable via induction on sn t1. Determinism is simpler.

So prove step_det. Let's think about a cleaner way: prove determinism only for terms that are locally closed? The step lemmas already require lc premises; maybe a restricted determinism is easier? But inversion handles both anyway.

Could we prove determinism via `functional` inversion? Perhaps use `dependent destruction`? Not available without Program. Let's just do it.

Actually, maybe we can prove a weaker lemma sufficient for sn_back: if step t t1 and step t t2 then sn t2 implies sn t1? Hmm, no.

Let's carefully write step_det by induction on the first step derivation.

The step constructors (from the file):
```
| ST_AppAbs : forall T t v,
    locally_closed (tm_abs T t) ->
    value v ->
    tm_app (tm_abs T t) v --> open t v
| ST_App1 : forall t1 t1' t2,
    t1 --> t1' ->
    locally_closed t2 ->
    tm_app t1 t2 --> tm_app t1' t2
| ST_App2 : forall v1 t2 t2',
    value v1 ->
    t2 --> t2' ->
    tm_app v1 t2 --> tm_app v1 t2'
```

Proof:
```
Lemma step_det : forall t t1 t2, step t t1 -> step t t2 -> t1 = t2.
Proof.
  intros t t1 t2 H1. revert t2.
  induction H1; intros t2 H2.
  - (* H1 = ST_AppAbs T t v Hlc Hv; t = app (abs T t) v; t1 = open t v *)
    inversion H2; subst; clear H2.
    + reflexivity.
    + (* ST_App1 case: H : step (abs T t) t1' *) exfalso.
      eapply value_no_step. apply v_abs. exact Hlc. exact H.
    + (* ST_App2: value (abs T t) -> step v t2' *) exfalso.
      eapply value_no_step. exact Hv. exact H.
```
Hmm, the naming after inversion: In the ST_AppAbs case, the inversion of H2 : step (tm_app (tm_abs T t) v) t2 yields three subgoals with parameters. Names may be H3 etc. We'll iterate with Coq.

For ST_App1 case of H1: H1 : step (tm_app t1 t2) (tm_app t1' t2) from Hstep : step t1 t1' and Hlc : lc t2. Our goal after revert t2 is `forall t2, step (tm_app t1 t2) t2 -> ...`? Hmm, `induction H1` will generalize appropriately. The IH for ST_App1 will be: forall (t2' : tm), step t1 t1' -> step t1 t2'?? I'm getting confused.

Let me think: Goal `forall t2, step (tm_app t1 t2) t2 = ...`? Actually after `intros t t1 t2 H1. revert t2. induction H1; intros t2 H2.` The goal before induction: `forall t2 : tm, step t t2 -> t1 = t2`? So step_det proof:

- Case ST_AppAbs: goal forall s, step (app (abs T body) v) s -> open body v = s. intro s H2; inversion H2; subst:

**Now, some deeper checks on the C/V approach.**

Concern: `C T t` includes `lc_at 0 t` and `sn t` and existence of value. In `C_app`, we use `V_arrow v1` where v1 is a value with `V (Arrow T1 T2) v1`; V includes lc v1 and forall u, C T1 u -> C T2(app v1 u). Good.

In T_Abs, to show V (Arrow T1 T2)(abs T1 body') we need lc abs and the forall u, C T1 u -> C T2(app abs u) property. We proved that using the IH at w (value reachable from u). Let's detail:

Given u with Hu : C T1 u:
- Hu gives Hlc_u, Hsn_u, and (w, Hmulti_u_w : multi step u w, Hval_w : value w, HV_w : V T1 w).
- HV_w and value w give C T1 w via `V_value_C`.
- IH_open applied to sigma' = update sigma x w, env_ok (update Gamma x T1) sigma':
   Need for all y S, update Gamma x T1 y = Some S -> C S (sigma' y).
   - y = x case: update Gamma x T1 x = Some T1; sigma' x = w; need C T1 w. Have.
   - y ≠ x: update Gamma x T1 y = Gamma y; sigma' y = sigma y; need C S (sigma y) from Henv.
   So get HC_open : C T2 (subst sigma' (open t1 (fvar x))).
- Commutation: subst sigma' (open t1 (fvar x)) = open body' w (where body' = subst sigma t1), using x ∉ fv t1 and subst_open_comm.
- So HC_open : C T2 (open body' w).
- Now C T2 (app (abs T1 body') u):
   * lc: lc_abs body' (need lc_at 1 body'; we have lc abs from earlier) and lc u.
   * sn: multi_app2 Hval_abs Hmulti_u_w gives multi step (app abs u) (app abs w). Wait multi_app2 requires `value v1` for the function: value (tm_abs T1 body') via v_abs with lc abs. Then step_abs: step (app abs w) (open body' w) via ST_AppAbs (lc abs) Hval_w. Compose: multi step (app abs u) (open body' w). HC_open gives sn(open body' w). sn_multi_back gives sn(app abs u).
   * exists value: HC_open gives z with open body' w -->* z, value z, V T2 z. Compose multistep: app abs u -->* open body' w -->* z. 
   So C T2 (app abs u). Good.
   
   Note: When u happens to be a value w = its own..., still fine.

Then C (Arrow T1 T2)(abs body') with the value witness abs body' and V proof. Good.

**The target theorem.**

```
Theorem normalization : forall t T, empty |-- t \in T -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T H.
  (* Apply fundamental with sigma = identity? or any sigma *)
```
We know sem_type empty t T: forall sigma, env_ok empty sigma -> C T (subst sigma t). Choose sigma = fun x => tm_fvar x? Then subst sigma t = t (subst identity lemma). env_ok empty sigma: forall x S, empty x = Some S -> ...; empty x = None, so vacuous.
Then C T t gives sn t. 

Alternatively choose sigma = fun _ => tm_true; subst sigma t = t if t has no fvars; but proving requires no_fvar. Identity substitution is easier: subst id t = t by `subst_id`. Need lemma `subst_id : forall t, subst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t = t`. Induction on t. Good.
env_ok empty id: intro x S H; unfold empty in H; discriminate.
So:
```
  assert (Hsem := fundamental _ _ _ H (fun x => tm_fvar x) env_empty).
  simpl in Hsem? 
  rewrite subst_id in Hsem.
  apply C_sn in Hsem.
```
Where fundamental is the proven lemma.

Also we should double check: `empty` is defined as fun _ => None. env_ok empty sigma: intros x S H; simpl in H; discriminate H.

**Now let's consider whether the C/V definition is well-formed as a Fixpoint.**

I'll write:
```
Fixpoint CV (T : ty) : (tm -> Prop) * (tm -> Prop) :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool =>
      (fun t => lc_at 0 t /\ (t = tm_true \/ t = tm_false),
       fun t => lc_at 0 t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
                 (exists v, multi step t v /\ value v /\
                    (lc_at 0 v /\ (v = tm_true \/ v = tm_false))))
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      let C1 := snd (CV T1) in
      let C2 := snd (CV T2) in
      let Varr := fun t => lc_at 0 t /\ (forall u, C1 u -> C2 (tm_app t u)) in
      (Varr,
       fun t => lc_at 0 t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
                 (exists v, multi step t v /\ value v /\ Varr v))
  end.

Definition V (T : ty) : tm -> Prop := fst (CV T).
Definition C (T : ty) : tm -> Prop := snd (CV T).
```

So define:
```
Definition env_ok (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=

  (forall x S, Gamma x = Some S -> C S (sigma x)).
```

**Typing_lc still needs freshness** relative to L. And T_Abs fundamental needs freshness relative to L and fv t1. Both use `fresh`.

Let's define fv and fresh:

```
Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom := ...
Definition fresh (L : list atom) : atom := S (fold_right Nat.max 0 L).
Lemma fresh_gt : forall L x, In x L -> x < fresh L.
Lemma fresh_fresh : forall L, ~ In (fresh L) L.
```

Note: `fold_right` from List. `Nat.max`. Imports: Lists.List gives fold_right. PeanoNat gives Nat.max, Nat.leb etc. We'll use `Nat.max`.

Proof of fresh_gt:
```
Lemma fold_max_bound : forall L x, In x L -> x <= fold_right Nat.max 0 L.
Proof.
  induction L as [|a L IH]; intros x Hin. contradiction.
  simpl. destruct Hin as [-> | Hin].
  - apply Nat.le_max_l.
  - eapply Nat.le_trans; [apply IH; exact Hin | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.
Lemma fresh_gt : forall L x, In x L -> x < fresh L.
Proof. intros. unfold fresh. apply Nat.lt_succ_r? x <= max... and max <= S max. lia. Qed.
```

**subst_ext statement:**
```
Lemma subst_ext : forall sigma sigma' t,
  (forall x, In x (fv t) -> sigma x = sigma' x) ->
  subst sigma t = subst sigma' t.
```
Proof induction t:
- bvar: subst = bvar; reflexivity.
- fvar x: subst sigma (fvar x) = sigma x; subst sigma' (fvar x) = sigma' x; apply H; simpl; auto.
- app: simpl; rewrite IH1, IH2; H conditions: In x (fv t1 ++ fv t2) ↔ ... Use `apply In_app_iff`. Actually the hypothesis is forall x, In x (fv (app t1 t2)) -> ...; fv (app) = fv t1 ++ fv t2. IH1 needs forall x, In x (fv t1) -> sigma x = sigma' x; intro x Hx; apply H; apply in_app_iff; left; exact Hx. IH2 similar right.
- abs: fv same; simpl; rewrite IH; apply H? The hypothesis for body is the same. `apply IH; exact H`? IH : (forall x, In x (fv t1) -> sigma x = sigma' x) -> subst sigma t1 = subst sigma' t1. Goal after simpl: tm_abs T (subst sigma t1) = tm_abs T (subst sigma' t1). apply f_equal; apply IH; exact H (with fv (abs) = fv t1 definitionally). Good.
- true/false: reflexivity.

Then:
```
Lemma subst_update_ext : forall sigma x v t, ~ In x (fv t) -> subst (update sigma x v) t = subst sigma t.
Proof.
  intros. apply subst_ext. intros y Hy. 
  (* need update sigma x v y = sigma y; since y ≠ x *)
  assert (y <> x) by (intro E; subst; apply H; exact Hy).
  symmetry? update_eq/neq: update sigma x v y = sigma y when x ≠ y.
  apply update_neq. auto.
Qed.
```
Careful direction: subst (update sigma x v) t = subst sigma t requires for y ∈ fv t: update sigma x v y = sigma y. `subst_ext` with sigma := update..., sigma' := sigma. update_neq sigma x y v : x <> y -> update sigma x v y = sigma y. We have y ≠ x; need x ≠ y. auto.

**subst_open_comm:**
```
Lemma subst_open_comm : forall sigma k u t,
  subst sigma (open_rec k u t) = open_rec k (subst sigma u) (subst sigma t).
Proof.
  intros sigma k u t. revert k u. induction t; intros k u; simpl.
  - (* bvar *) destruct (Nat.eqb k n); reflexivity.
  - (* fvar *) reflexivity.
  - (* app *) simpl; rewrite IHt1, IHt2; reflexivity.
  - (* abs *) simpl. rewrite IHt. reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
Qed.

But `simpl` might not reduce `subst sigma (if ...)`, so we need destruct first. Let's structure:
```

```
Actually `simpl` on subst may unfold the fixpoint only when the term is a constructor; for `open_rec` also. Let's write case bvar with destruct. Fine.

In the abs case: IHt : forall k u, subst sigma (open_rec k u t) = open_rec k (subst sigma u)(subst sigma t). Goal: subst sigma (open_rec (S k) u (abs T t)) = open_rec (S k) (subst sigma u) (subst sigma (abs T t)). LHS = subst sigma (abs T (open_rec (S k) u t)) = abs T (subst sigma (open_rec (S k) u t)); RHS = abs T (open_rec (S k) (subst sigma u) (subst sigma t)). Apply f_equal, IHt.

Lemma lc_open_back : forall k u t, lc_at k (open_rec k u t) -> lc_at (S k) t.

  - apply lc_false.

**Fundamental theorem: environment and T_Abs.**

Let's write sem_type and prove.

```
Definition sem_type (Gamma : context) (t : tm) (T : ty) : Prop :=
  forall sigma,
    (forall x, lc_at 0 (sigma x)) ->
    (forall x S, Gamma x = Some S -> C S (sigma x)) ->
    C T (subst sigma t).
```
Including lc explicitly (rather than a record).

Actually, combining into env_ok is cleaner:
```
Definition env_ok (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  (forall x, lc_at 0 (sigma x)) /\ (forall x S, Gamma x = Some S -> C S (sigma x)).
Definition sem_type Gamma t T := forall sigma, env_ok Gamma sigma -> C T (subst sigma t).
```

T_Abs proof needs to construct env_ok (update Gamma x T1) (update sigma x w):
- lc: forall y, lc_at 0 (update sigma x w y). destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E: if true then w (C_lc), else sigma y (env lc).
  `unfold update. destruct (Nat.eqb x y); [apply C_lc; exact HCw | apply Henv_lc].`
- domain: as discussed.

Let's write helper lemmas for env_ok update:
```

```

Also in T_Var: env gives C T (sigma x) since Gamma x = Some T; subst = sigma x.

We also need `Heq` carefully:
`subst sigma' (open t1 (fvar x)) = open (subst sigma t1) w = open body w`.
- subst_open_comm sigma' 0 (tm_fvar x) t1: subst sigma' (open_rec 0 (fvar x) t1) = open_rec 0 (subst sigma' (fvar x)) (subst sigma' t1).

Code chunk 1:

```coq
(** * Auxiliary definitions and lemmas for the normalization proof. *)

Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  end.

Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  end.

Definition fresh (L : list atom) : atom := S (fold_right Nat.max 0 L).
```

Then lemmas.

Let me be careful with `Nat.max` and `fold_right`: `fold_right` has implicit A, B; `fold_right Nat.max 0 L` should infer.

`fresh_gt`:
```
Lemma fold_right_max_bound : forall L x,
  In x L -> x <= fold_right Nat.max 0 L.
Proof.
  induction L as [|a L IH]; intros x Hin.
  - inversion Hin.
  - simpl in Hin. destruct Hin as [-> | Hin].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + eapply Nat.le_trans; [apply IH; exact Hin | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_gt : forall L x, In x L -> x < fresh L.
Proof.
  intros. unfold fresh. pose proof (fold_right_max_bound L x H). lia.
Qed.

Lemma fresh_fresh : forall L, ~ In (fresh L) L.
Proof.
  intros L H. pose proof (fresh_gt L (fresh L) H). lia.
Qed.
```

Excellent! All auxiliary lemmas up to typing_lc compile. Now we're at the theorem with an empty proof (expected). Now let's build the CV logical relation and the fundamental theorem.

Let's write the CV definition and characteristic lemmas.

```
(** ** Logical relation *)

Fixpoint CV (T : ty) : (tm -> Prop) * (tm -> Prop) :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool =>
      (fun t => lc_at 0 t /\ (t = tm_true \/ t = tm_false),
       fun t => lc_at 0 t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
                 (exists v, multi step t v /\ value v /\
                    (lc_at 0 v /\ (v = tm_true \/ v = tm_false))))
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      let C1 := snd (CV T1) in
      let C2 := snd (CV T2) in
      let Varr := fun t => lc_at 0 t /\ (forall u, C1 u -> C2 (tm_app t u)) in
      (Varr,
       fun t => lc_at 0 t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
                 (exists v, multi step t v /\ value v /\ Varr v))
  end.

Definition V (T : ty) : tm -> Prop := fst (CV T).
Definition C (T : ty) : tm -> Prop := snd (CV T).
```

Characteristic lemmas: Let's try:
```
Lemma V_Bool : forall t, V Ty_Bool t <-> lc_at 0 t /\ (t = tm_true \/ t = tm_false).
Proof. intros. unfold V, CV. cbn. split; intros H; exact H. Qed.
```

Let me write the fundamental theorem. Plan:

```
Definition env_ok (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  (forall x, lc_at 0 (sigma x)) /\
  (forall x S, Gamma x = Some S -> C S (sigma x)).

Definition sem_type (Gamma : context) (t : tm) (T : ty) : Prop :=
  forall sigma, env_ok Gamma sigma -> C T (subst sigma t).

Lemma fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  has_type Gamma t T -> sem_type Gamma t T.
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T H.
  induction H as [G x S Heq
                 | L G T1 T2 t1 Hprem IHprem
                 | G a b T1 T2 Ha IHa Hb IHb
                 | G
                 | G].
  - (* T_Var *) unfold sem_type. intros sigma [Hlcs Henv]. simpl.
    apply Henv. exact Heq.
  - (* T_Abs *)
    unfold sem_type. intros sigma [Hlcs Henv]. simpl.
    set (body := subst sigma t1).
    (* fresh x *)
    pose (x := fresh (L ++ fv t1)).
    assert (HxL : ~ In x L).
    { intro HIn. apply (fresh_fresh (L ++ fv t1)). apply in_app_iff. left. exact HIn. }
    assert (Hxfv : ~ In x (fv t1)).
    { intro HIn. apply (fresh_fresh (L ++ fv t1)). apply in_app_iff. right. exact HIn. }
    (* get premise at x *)
    assert (Hpx : has_type (update G x T1) (open t1 (tm_fvar x)) T2)
      by (apply Hprem; exact HxL).
    (* lc of body *)
    assert (Hlc1 : lc_at 1 t1).
    { apply lc_open_back with (k := 0) (u := tm_fvar x).
      apply typing_lc. exact Hpx. }
    assert (Hlcbody : lc_at 1 body).
    { unfold body. apply subst_lc with (k := 1); [exact Hlc1 | exact Hlcs]. }
    assert (Hlcabs : lc_at 0 (tm_abs T1 body)) by (apply lc_abs; exact Hlcbody).
    (* show C *)
    apply C_intro.
    + exact Hlcabs.
    + apply sn_abs.
    + exists (tm_abs T1 body). split.
      * apply multi_refl.
      * split.
        -- apply v_abs. exact Hlcabs.
        -- apply (proj2 (V_Arrow T1 T2 (tm_abs T1 body))).
           split.
           ++ exact Hlcabs.
           ++ intros u Hu.
              destruct (C_elim _ _ Hu) as (Hlcu & Hsnu & Hexu).
              destruct Hexu as (w & Hmuw & Hvalw & HVw).
              assert (HCw : C T1 w) by (apply V_value_C; assumption).
              (* apply IHprem x HxL with sigma' = env_update sigma x w *)
              assert (Hsem : sem_type (update G x T1) (open t1 (tm_fvar x)) T2)
                by (apply IHprem; exact HxL).
              assert (HCopen : C T2 (subst (env_update sigma x w) (open t1 (tm_fvar x)))).
              { apply Hsem. unfold env_ok. split.
                - intros y. unfold env_update. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
                  + apply C_lc in HCw. exact HCw.
                  + apply Hlcs.
                - intros y S Hy. unfold update in Hy.
                  destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
                  + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst y. injection Hy as HyS. subst S. exact HCw.
                  + apply Henv. exact Hy. }
              (* rewrite to open body w *)
              assert (Heq : subst (env_update sigma x w) (open t1 (tm_fvar x))
                            = open body w).
              { unfold body. rewrite (subst_open_comm (env_update sigma x w) 0 (tm_fvar x) t1).
                - simpl. rewrite subst_env_update_ext.
                  + reflexivity.
